# Suggestions

A running list of actionable improvements surfaced during /update-docs runs.
Each item is outside the scope of the work that surfaced it. Strike through when done.

---

## Backfill ledger

Standing list of **new learnings that may apply to EXISTING/shipped work** — so they survive across sessions and aren't silently forgotten. Per [[learning-propagation-protocol]]: a candidate here is reworked ONLY after a 360 analysis + explicit user permission. Status: *identified → analysed → awaiting-permission → approved / declined → done*.

| Learning | Existing-work candidate | Status (2026-06-10) |
|---|---|---|
| **A question can LOSE a required context at ingest, and the RULE 1 exclusion is structurally blind to it — the filter drops the sibling that survived and admits the one that broke. Found 2026-09-02 while reviewing Blueprint Mock 5.** In `NDA_Maths_Mock_Test_01.docx` ONE frequency table serves Q113 and Q114; at ingest the table was attached as `context` to **Q114 only**. So Q114 (intact) is correctly excluded by the builder's set/context-bound filter, while **Q113 (unanswerable) passes it as a clean standalone row** — `set_id`, `context` and `image_url` all null, invisible to every gate. RULE 1 tests for a context that IS PRESENT; it cannot see one that is REQUIRED AND MISSING, so the predicate points the wrong way for this failure mode. | **Exactly TWO rows, both PUBLIC, both LWS mock `.docx`** — measured, not estimated: a first probe matched 145 and was almost entirely false positives (*"which of the above statements"* is the standard NDA phrasing for a statement list carried in the stem itself). (1) `38a90f9a-4baa-47b4-8aa8-44cdcb79aae8` (Mock_Test_01 Q113) — **recoverable**, the table is already in the DB on Q114 and can be copied across. (2) `f75633b1-a8b0-4626-8aa6-ed9b52fd85e9` (Mock_Test_02 Q80, *"…is given above"*) — **worse**: neighbours Q79/Q81 are unrelated, so the table is nowhere in the DB. Recovery needs the source `.docx`, and a 2026-09-02 search of `C:\tmp`, `C:\Vilas`, Downloads, Documents, Desktop and OneDrive did NOT find any of the 14 `NDA_Maths_Mock_Test_*.docx` files (they were uploaded through the web `/upload` path, so no tracked script references them either) — so this row may not be recoverable at all, and the honest options are withdraw-to-PRIVATE or leave-and-flag. **`context` IS part of `subjectiveContentHash` but these are MCQs**, so check `contentHash` before writing — a context edit may move row identity, which is the difference between an UPDATE and a delete-and-re-commit. No PYQ or textbook row is affected. Worth deciding separately whether the builder should also gain a positive probe (stem references external data + no context ⇒ refuse), since fixing two rows leaves the blind spot open for the next ingest. | **identified** (2026-09-02) |
| **A disclosure stored in `pyq_note` renders INSIDE the bracket the /browse card draws, so it ends in a visible `]]` — and the field is the wrong home for it anyway. Found 2026-09-02 by looking at a rendered card, after every gate was green.** `QuestionCard.tsx` renders the provenance line as `[{provenance}]`, and `formatProvenance` puts `pyq_note` in it verbatim. CDS General Knowledge stores its derived-answer disclosure as `CDS (I) 2019 — General Knowledge [No official answer key is published for this paper. …]`, so the inner bracket nests. Beyond the cosmetics, the placement is wrong on the merits: `pyq_note` has exactly one consumer, the SOURCE line, and a reader looking at the QUESTION has not seen an answer yet — there is nothing for them to mistake for an official key. The structured signal `derived_model` already exists on these rows and never reaches the UI. | **2,280 PUBLIC CDS General Knowledge rows** (every row of the corpus; all 19 papers 2016-II…2026-I). Also worth checking in the same pass: `mh-ssc-10-text` and `mh-sb-9`, which stamp a `pyq_note` clause under the same pattern — their exact wording has not been measured, so whether they nest is unverified rather than assumed. The repair is a one-field text UPDATE scoped by `source_file`; **`pyq_note` is not part of `content_hash`**, so unlike a stem repair it cannot move row identity, orphan a paper ref or stale a `question_reviews` verdict. The real decision is not the brackets but whether the disclosure belongs on the card at all — and for CDS the answer may genuinely differ from the Physics one, because CDS is EXAM PAPERS where 'official answer key' is a real artifact students hunt for, which is exactly the premise that does not hold for a textbook. | **Identified 2026-09-02 while opening the State Board Physics lane; NOT fixed — user's call to review CDS separately.** The Physics chapter took the opposite decision the same day (`pyq_note` = source only; the derived-answer fact kept as `derived_model`/`derived_at` and the flip-public gate re-keyed onto that column rather than a prose match). Whether CDS should follow is a product judgement, not a cleanup — hence a 360 and explicit permission before touching 2,280 shipped rows. |
| **`pyq_note` is free text typed into an uploaded spreadsheet, and at least one of them is provably wrong** — found while building the MHT-CET mock registry (2026-09-01). `MHT_CET_3rdMay2023_S1_QB.xlsx` stores the note **"3rd May 2nd Shift"**, contradicting its own filename, while `MHT_CET_3rdMay2023_Shift2_QuestionBank.xlsx` already legitimately claims that sitting — so taken at its word the bank has two files for 3rd May Shift 2 and none for Shift 1. Settled against the local source booklets rather than by preferring the more official-looking field: stems unique to that file ("watch glass", "coloured compound") appear ONLY in `3 may shift 1 (ques).docx`, and the Shift-2 file's stems only in `3 may shift 2` — the **FILENAME is right and the note is a typo**. | **~150 live PUBLIC rows carry the wrong shift label** in `pyq_note`. It is provenance metadata, so nothing computes from it today and the mocks deliberately take their labels from `scripts/mocks/mhtcetSittings.ts` instead — but it IS rendered as the source citation on `/browse`, so a student comparing two shifts sees one paper filed under the other's sitting. The repair is a one-row-value `UPDATE` scoped by `source_file`, and — worth checking first — **`pyq_note` is not part of `content_hash`**, so unlike a stem repair this does NOT change row identity and needs no delete-and-re-commit. Also worth a wider sweep while in there: 3 other MHT-CET sources carry undated notes ("Shift 1", "Shift \|\|") whose sitting nobody has established. | **Identified 2026-09-01; NOT fixed.** Shipped content, so it needs a 360 + explicit permission. Low risk and low blast radius, but it is a data edit to live rows and the wider sweep needs the same source-booklet verification this one got — a *plausible* date is exactly what must not be written. |
| **The GAT content rules were written down on 2026-08-26 and made enforceable (`scripts/practice-paper/GAT_RULES.md` + `gat-lint.ts`) — which means every GAT paper ingested BEFORE that date was ingested without them.** Rules 1-3 are structural: statement lists (`1.` `2.` `3.`) each need their own line, `P./Q./R./S.` rearrangement parts each need their own line, and a List I / List II or any two-column pair list must be a GFM pipe table (header row AND `|---|---|` separator, or the renderer treats it as prose). The dominant cause is a one-line normalisation habit — collapsing all whitespace (`\s+` -> `" "`) flattens exactly these three shapes while being correct for ordinary prose, whose source newlines really are just wrap points. | **101 BLOCKING findings across 11 shipped GAT papers**, measured by `gat-lint.ts` after two of its own false positives were found and fixed (a correct match-list table's cells look like a flattened statement list; "which pair is not correctly matched" puts its pairs in the OPTIONS, so there is nothing to tabulate — that pass took the count 131 -> 104 -> 101). Spread: `gat-mock-w09` 20, `gat-mock-3` 20, `gat-mock-w011` 18, `gat-mock-7` 13, `gat-mock-21aug` 8, `gat-mock-w07` 7, `gat-mock-w2` 5, `gat-mock-g1` 5, `gat-mock-4` 4, `gat-mock-w03` 3, `gat-mock-w1` 1. Almost all are rule 1 and rule 2. **Cheap and low-risk in principle** — these are presentation defects in `stem` text, not answer defects — but `content_hash` covers the stem, so a repair changes a row's identity: it needs delete-and-re-commit rather than an UPDATE, which mints fresh ids and would orphan any paper ref or authored-solution pairing. A handful are rule 7 (a stem referencing a figure that does not exist), which are NOT presentation defects and are unpublishable rather than untidy. | **Identified 2026-08-26 while building the lint; NOT fixed.** Shipped work, so it needs a 360 + explicit permission. Note the fix is not uniform: the rule-1/2/3 rows are cosmetic-but-hash-changing, while the rule-7 rows are genuine correctness problems and should be triaged separately rather than swept in the same pass. |
| **`verify-commit.ts` reports a FALSE FAILURE for any paper with `createPaper:false` — found 2026-08-26.** That mode commits only `status:"new"` rows (documented in `gat-mock-g1`'s own config comment: *"dup rows ... are never committed — createPaper:false commits new only"*), but the gate's row-count and question-number checks compare the DB against ALL records in the file. So a correct ingest fails, and the failure is indistinguishable from a real one. | **Reproduced on shipped data**: `gat-mock-g1` fails the same two checks today, so this is pre-existing rather than introduced by the Oswaal Mock 10 ingest, which fails three (row count, question-number set, and the shared-passage set — the third because a cloze member held back as `flawed` legitimately leaves 4 of 5 members carrying the context). The fix is to make the gate aware of the mode: expect `new`-only rows when `createPaper` is false, and scope the passage check to committed rows. **The danger of leaving it is habituation** — a gate that always fails stops being read, which is exactly how a real failure gets waved through. | **Identified 2026-08-26 during the Mock 10 commit; NOT fixed.** Shipped tooling, so it needs a 360 + permission. Mock 10's invariants were verified directly by SQL instead (128 rows, all `practice`, all PRIVATE, 0 null solutions/subtopics/chapters, 0 rows with other than 4 options, 0 with other than 1 correct, 0 duplicate options, 4 cloze rows sharing 1 `set_id`) — which is what the gate would have asserted had it been mode-aware. |
| **The bank contains its own near-duplicate pairs — surfaced 2026-08-26 by a dedup pass that was pointed OUTWARD.** While matching 1,350 Oswaal mock questions against the NDA corpus, the scorer repeatedly returned two bank rows for one incoming question. | **Confirmed pairs so far: troposphere, silver tarnishing, acid rain (one Chemistry row and one Geography row with identical options), and temporary hardness of water.** This is a small sample from a scan that was not looking for it, so the real count is unknown and probably larger. The tooling to measure it already exists — `scripts/practice-paper/dump-bank.ts --subject` plus the same IDF-cosine matcher — pointed at the bank against itself rather than against an incoming paper. Note one of the pairs spans two SUBJECTS, so a per-subject sweep would miss it. | **Identified 2026-08-26; NOT investigated or fixed.** Out of scope for the Oswaal ingest, which only needed to know whether an incoming question was already present. Worth its own measured pass before deciding anything: an intra-bank duplicate is not automatically wrong (the Decisions log records a deliberate keep-duplicates-as-recurrence-signal policy for PYQs), so the question is which of these are genuine redundancy in the PRACTICE corpus. |
| **377 CDS English questions reference a passage that was never stored — found 2026-08-23.** Reading Comprehension 223 of 268 (83%) and Cloze Test 154 of 174 (89%) carry a `context` that is only a stub: `Directions: answer from the passage / Passage / (Passage not stored — refer to the source booklet: CDS (I) 2018 English, items Q51-55.)`. The questions and their directions were transcribed; the text they are ABOUT was not. As stored, all 377 are **unanswerable** — a blind deriver can only abstain, and a student handed one has nothing to read. No gate catches it: `context` is non-null and non-empty, the options are well-formed, exactly one is keyed, and `audit:text` checks long-form defects rather than whether a referenced passage exists. It stayed invisible because only the 45 RC and 20 Cloze rows that DO have passages were ever drawable into a paper. | **377 rows across 55 passages, spanning every sitting 2017-I to 2024-II** (RC 39 passages, Cloze 16). Recoverable rather than lost: all 19 source booklets are on disk at `C:	mp\PYQPs\CDS. English` (0.5–21 MB each, 2017-I → 2026-I), `scripts/cds/config.ts` already maps each `source_file` to its PDF, `scripts/cds/render.ts` rasterises them, and **each stub names its own item range**, so the passage-to-question mapping needs no reconstruction. The work is a vision transcription pass over 55 passages, then an update to `context` — which is NOT part of `subjectiveContentHash` for MCQs and not part of `contentHash` at all, so the repair cannot move a row's identity or orphan a paper ref. Worth pairing with a standing probe (`context ilike '%not stored%'`), which currently returns exactly these 377. | **Identified 2026-08-23 while scoping GAT mock 2; NOT fixed — user's call to finish the mock first.** Mock 2 needs only one Cloze passage and a surviving one exists, so the mock is unblocked. Doing it would unlock 377 questions including 39 genuine RC passages, which is what a future GAT mock needs for a real comprehension block — mock 1 consumed the ONLY stored RC passage in the corpus, and mock 2 substitutes Cloze because there is no second one. |
| **A solution that ASSERTS instead of DERIVING is a CORRECTNESS probe, not a readability one — measured 2026-08-25, and the ratio is the finding.** Sweeping NDA Maths for phrases that carry a conclusion with no executed step (`verified numerically`, `evaluating gives`, `it can be shown`, `the printed solution`, `by an appropriate`) flagged **79 of 5,347 PUBLIC solutions**. Rewriting them produced **62 rewrites, 2 legitimate keeps, and 15 REPORTs where the keyed answer could not be reached from the stem at all** — a 19% defect rate among flagged rows. Nearly every REPORT is a **mis-transcribed stem under a CORRECT key**, and the hand-waving was the symptom rather than the disease: the original author could not derive the printed answer from the corrupted stem either, so they named a method and asserted its result. **RULE 4's blind re-derivation cannot see this class** — the key is right, so the row simply AGREEs. One row proves why numeric spot-checks are banned as justification: the true and transcribed relations intersect at exactly one point, and that point is the obvious angle to test (45 degrees), so a check there agrees by coincidence and falsely confirms the wrong stem. | **ALL 14 REPORTed rows were adjudicated against the printed sources 2026-08-26 and the result inverted the prior.** The 14 split exactly by corpus, and the two halves needed OPPOSITE treatment. **10 practice rows: every key was already RIGHT** - 10/10 matched the booklet's own printed answer key, so not one was a wrong answer. Eight were OUR transcription corrupting the stem or the options (a stacked fraction (1/6)sin read as '1 - sin'; concrete vectors i+j-k symbolised into a+b, which is why the question appeared to state no data; 'two options each, namely true and false' read as 'three options each'; one option block shifted so our (A) held (b)'s value), and two are BOOK defects where our text is byte-faithful and the printed page is itself wrong. **4 PYQ rows: every key was WRONG** - all four stems byte-faithful to the scanned UPSC booklets, so the answer was the issue. The decisive fact is that **the booklets carry NO answer key at all** (their tail pages are SPACE FOR ROUGH WORK), so there was no issued key to preserve; the stored key came from a prep-house Excel whose Answer column sits beside plainly LLM-authored solutions - Q34's contains a literal '?' mid-derivation and Q77's ends 'REVIEW: official answer is 0; ... check problem statement'. That is a derivation, not a published key, so correcting it does not override UPSC. Keys moved A->C, C->D, B->D, B->D, each re-derived from the printed stem without consulting the stored key and verified symbolically and numerically (25 checks, 0 failures). **Reconstructions were wrong 3 times out of 4** where a rewriter offered one, which is the standing argument for reading the source rather than reasoning about it. | **DONE 2026-08-26 - all 14 applied, verified and recorded.** 10 stem/option repairs (keys untouched, and the script asserts the key it hashes with is the key it read) + 4 key corrections + 4 rewritten solutions naming the two book defects. Every repaired stem was proved to REACH its stored key before the write (`data/report-adjudication/verify-practice.py`), and the four PYQ answers independently before it (`verify-pyq.py`); before-state snapshots are committed beside them for rollback. `mock_attempts` stores a FROZEN score, so a key move does not re-grade - **one real student had picked C on Q34, the correct answer, and been marked wrong**; re-graded +3.33 via the new `regrade-attempt.ts`, which reuses the app's own `gradeMock` and REFUSES to lower a score without `--allow-lower`. Nobody lost marks. All 14 recorded in `question_reviews` as `blind_rederivation` under run `report-adjudication:2026-08-26-nda-maths`, split 8 `stem_fixed` / 2 `defect_preserved` / 4 `key_fixed` so 'we were wrong' and 'the source was wrong' stay distinguishable. Verified: 14/14 PUBLIC, exactly one correct option, 4 distinct options, solution present; render-check PASS on web and Word (258 math zones, 0 KaTeX, 0 OMML, 0 control chars); `audit:text` clean on all 6 practice sources; `audit:keys` and `audit:omml` flag 16 and 6 rows respectively, **none of them among the 14** (pre-existing, other chapters - logged below). Remaining from this sweep: the same hand-wave markers are unswept in every other subject and exam. **FOLLOW-UP 2026-08-26 — the 16 `audit:keys` rows surfaced by that pass are also RESOLVED, and NOT ONE was a wrong answer.** **11 had two options marked correct and the second mark was never spurious: every one is a genuine MULTIPLE-CORRECT question** (JEE-Advanced-style items reprinted in this booklet, whose stems say 'is/are', 'statement(s)', 'value(s) is (are)'), each second option verified TRUE by solving. The defect is that this bank models exactly one correct option and `question_format` has no multi-correct member, so the only single-answer repair available is to un-mark a TRUE option - which marks a student wrong for a correct answer. Withdrawn to PRIVATE (visibility only; ids, text and paper refs intact, reversible in one UPDATE). **3 flagged SOLN != KEY were PROBE FALSE POSITIVES** - each solution already carried an adjudicated `[Textbook note: the source book keys option D ...]` bracket and the probe read that letter as the solution's own conclusion; reworded to name the option's TEXT, which silences the probe permanently instead of relocating it. **2 duplicate-option rows are BOTH the BOOK's own duplicates**, verified on the page, and were treated differently by HARM: Q2280 duplicates a distractor (key unique - preserved PUBLIC with the defect named) while Q2517 duplicates the ANSWER (a student choosing the twin is right and marked wrong - withdrawn). Measured bank-wide afterwards: **0 PUBLIC rows with more than one correct option across all 13 exams**, and JEE Mains has none by construction (Mains is single-correct; multi-correct is JEE Advanced, which is not ingested). All 16 recorded as `defect_preserved` under run `audit-keys-adjudication:2026-08-26-nda-maths-practice`. **Newly surfaced and NOT fixed: 5 PUBLIC CBSE Class 12 board-PYQ MCQs have four options and ZERO marked correct** - a different defect class in a different corpus, logged as its own candidate below. |
| **An MCQ with FOUR options and ZERO marked correct is invisible to every gate this repo runs - found 2026-08-26.** `audit:keys` is scoped to `question_kind='practice'`, `board:lint` checks section structure, `audit:text` checks long-form text defects, and a NULL solution is legal - so nothing asks the plainest question of all: does this MCQ have an answer? A sweep of the whole bank for `count(is_correct) <> 1` on non-subjective, non-numeric rows found **5 PUBLIC CBSE Class 12 board-PYQ MCQs with four options and no correct option at all**. On `/browse` the reveal shows no answer; in a mock the grader would find no key and mark every attempt wrong with a penalty - the same failure mode the CDS dangling-ref case documents. | **5 rows, all `cbse-12-pyq` (ingested 2026-08-21), all PUBLIC:** 2023 65-3-2 Q10 (probability of A and B), 2023 65-5-1 Q12 (sum of order and degree), 2023 65-2-1 Q18 (LPP corner points), 2025 65-6-1 Q16 (range of |lambda a|), 2025 65-2-1 Q6 (direction angles). Each needs its answer derived and source-verified against that paper's official CBSE marking scheme, which is the corpus's established source of record - so this is per-row adjudication, not a bulk flip. **Worth checking at the same time whether the cause is systematic**: all five are MCQs in a corpus whose ingest notes record that CBSE marking schemes must be read as IMAGES because the text layer yields no math, so a key that failed to parse would plausibly leave exactly this shape. If so the right fix is a commit-time guard that REFUSES an MCQ with no correct option, not five hand fixes. | **Identified 2026-08-26 while resolving the `audit:keys` flags; NOT fixed - different corpus, different source of record, and newly-discovered scope.** Deliberately surfaced rather than bulk-repaired: deriving five board answers without the marking scheme would put a guess where the corpus promises a verified key. The standing probe to add is one SQL line - `count(*) filter (where is_correct) <> 1` over non-subjective, non-numeric PUBLIC rows - which currently returns exactly these 5 and would have caught them at ingest. |
| **Restoring a missing passage makes a previously-underivable key CHECKABLE for the first time — found 2026-08-26 during the CDS passage backfill.** CDS English ships no official answer key, so every key in that corpus is LLM-derived. For the 367 questions whose passage was never transcribed, that derivation was made **without the text the question is about** — the deriver had only the stem and four options. Restoring the passage does not merely make the question answerable by a student; it supplies, for the first time, the evidence needed to check the stored key. Five suspected wrong keys surfaced as a by-product of transcribing seven papers, without anyone looking for them. | **Five rows flagged by transcription agents, each with the passage sentence that contradicts the key.** `2017-2 Q54` — the passage states the iceberg "turns upside down, it appears **green**", option (a) is `green`, stored key is **C = blue**. `2018-1 Q104` — the booklet's option (a) is **`prestige`** ("to regain their independence and old ___ in 1817") while `questions.json` stores `attempt` and keys **C = rebellion**; this is the documented CDS mis-slotted-option class and is a live wrong answer. `2018-2 Q36` — key **D** ("complete failure of the government") is unsupported; the passage's only grounds point at option (c). `2017-2 Q63` and `2021-2 Q18` — arguable rather than clearly wrong, flagged at lower confidence. Also two option-transcription drifts on `2018-1 Q91`/`Q94` (a dropped option, an invented one, and a shifted set) that do not move the answer. | **Identified 2026-08-26 during the passage backfill; NOT fixed — deliberately out of scope.** The backfill's remit is the `passage` field, which is hash-neutral; a key flip or an option repair changes `content_hash`, mints a new row and needs the resync/re-flip sequence, so it is a different repair with a different blast radius. Worth doing as one pass **after** all 58 passages are restored, because the same evidence will surface more: only 7 of 15 papers had been transcribed when these five appeared. The right shape is a blind re-derivation over every passage-dependent question now that the passages exist — which is exactly the control that was unavailable before, and which `question_reviews` can record as `blind_rederivation` to distinguish "we were wrong" from "the source was wrong". |
| **Multi-statement stems render as one run-on line, and the convention is applied inconsistently across the bank.** Found 2026-08-25 while reviewing NDA Maths Blueprint Mock 1. A stem presenting two labelled claims (`Statement-1: ... Statement-2: ...`) reads as a single paragraph unless real newlines separate them — which matters more on a PRINTED paper than on the web, because the student cannot re-flow it. Both surfaces already honour a real newline (`KatexRenderer` sets `whiteSpace: "pre-wrap"`; docx `mathRuns()` splits on the newline character (``) and emits `TextRun({break:1})`), so this is purely a stored-text defect. **Measured NDA Maths PUBLIC: 13 stems carry 2+ inline `Statement-N:` labels, and 7 more already have the newlines** — i.e. the bank disagrees with itself. Separately, 11 rows use tabular language with no GFM table; most are the deliberate linearizations CLAUDE.md records as future-only, so they are NOT automatically defects. | **12 rows outside Mock 1** (6 PYQ + 7 practice, minus the 1 repaired here). The repair is hash-neutral by construction — `contentHash` normalises runs of whitespace (`\s+`) to a single space — so it cannot orphan a row, break `paper_questions` refs, or stale a `question_reviews` verdict, which makes this unusually low-risk. Tooling exists: `scripts/reviews/apply-formatting-fixes.ts` takes a paper id, refuses any edit whose hash moves, and dry-runs by default; it would need widening from a paper to a `source_file` or subject scope. **Before any sweep, the 11 tabular-language rows need triage first** — deciding which are genuine flattened tables and which are the documented Statistics/Match-List linearizations that were deliberately left alone. | **Identified 2026-08-25.** Logged, not fixed: the 12 are shipped PUBLIC rows outside the paper under review, so they go through the normal 360 + permission gate rather than riding along in an unrelated commit. The 1 row inside Mock 1 (Q668) WAS fixed, since it is part of the deliverable being reviewed. |
| **A BLIND RE-DERIVATION CANNOT CATCH A CORRUPTED OPTION SET — measured 2026-08-25, and it invalidates the assumption behind the CDS verification plan.** The blind protocol (solve from stem+options without seeing the key, then compare letters) is this repo's strongest key control. It is silently defeated when the OPTION TEXT is itself mis-transcribed, because the solver derives the right answer, looks for the label carrying that text, and confirms whichever label holds it. On CDS the transcriber had repeatedly copied the correct option's text into the WRONG letter's slot, so the blind pass confirmed the wrong letter — deriving correctly and concluding wrongly. **Proof, not inference: `question_reviews` holds `bank-paper:cds-english-blind-2026-08-23`, 89 rows across 8 papers, every one `verdict='confirmed'` — and one of them, 2021-2 Q59, is a key this session proved wrong (A→C) by reading the printed page.** The blind pass saw our option (a) holding the text "S R Q P", which IS the correct ordering, and confirmed (a); the booklet prints "P Q R S" at (a) and "S R Q P" at (c). | **Transcription fidelity is a PREREQUISITE control, not a parallel one, and the two must be ordered.** Re-deriving keys over a corrupted option set launders a transcription error into a confirmed verdict — which is worse than no review, because the verdict is then cited as evidence. The cheap decisive check is per-question and needs no solving: compare our option SET against the printed page (catches a wrong option) **and separately our label→text ORDER** (catches a swap or rotation, which leaves four distinct strings and is invisible to set comparison, to duplicate detection, and to `audit:keys`). Measured on 2025-2's match-list block: **6 of 20 rows corrupted, only 1 of the 6 had any duplicate**, and 3 carried live wrong keys. Practical consequence for the standing CDS backlog: **the 89 `confirmed` rows from the 2026-08-23 run should be treated as UNVERIFIED until their option sets have been checked against the source**, and any future blind pass on a vision-transcribed corpus should be gated behind an option-fidelity pass. | **Identified 2026-08-25 from a proven counter-example.** The 22 rows found this session are repaired and their keys corrected under assertions (`scripts/cds/apply-key-fixes.ts`); the match-list sections of the remaining papers were swept. Not yet done: re-validating the other 88 rows of the 2026-08-23 blind run, and deciding whether the same ordering rule applies to the other vision-transcribed corpora (the practice bank, Foundation, mh-ssc-10) — none of which has had an option-ORDER check either. |
| **19 CDS English mock tests now GRADE STUDENTS against answer keys nobody has verified.** CDS booklets ship with no official key, so all 2,280 answers are LLM-derived and self-confidence-flagged: **1,423 HIGH · 648 MED · 132 LOW · 77 carrying no marker at all**. `scripts/cds/README.md`'s own gate said "human spot-check before flipping PUBLIC"; on 2026-08-25 that gate was deliberately deferred by product decision so the mocks could ship, and the corpus was flipped PUBLIC as-is. This is a *known, accepted* debt, not an oversight — logged here because the deferral is otherwise recorded only in one conversation. What makes it sharper than an ordinary unverified corpus: a wrong key on `/browse` shows a student a wrong answer, whereas a wrong key in a mock **deducts marks under −1/3 negative marking** and feeds `mock_attempts` scores the student is told to trust. The confidence labels are also self-assessed by the transcribing agent, and the 2026 trial already showed a section split silently flipped 5 answers that would have read HIGH — so HIGH is not a verification, it is an opinion. | **Blind re-derive all 2,280 CDS keys** — the repo's established strongest control (the JEE BLIND lane, Worksheets' dual-blind, the NCERT MCQ passes), where agents solve from stem+options WITHOUT seeing the stored key and disagreements go to a second independent blind pass. That is the only method that catches a *stealth* wrong key, i.e. one whose solution is internally consistent and simply wrong — the 2026-06-03 audit's central finding, and by construction invisible to a read-through. Priority order is measurable: worst papers first by uncertain-row count — **2025-2 (62 uncertain), 2017-1 (60), 2024-2 (58), 2018-1 (55), 2022-2 (53)**; cleanest is 2026-1 (24, of which 15 are merely unmarked). Only a resulting KEY FLIP touches shipped rows and needs the usual adjudication. |

| **A printed answer key recorded in `config.ts` for the step-6 cross-check is the SAME BYTES as a key that must be withheld from the blind derivation — and `scripts/mh-sb-9/TRANSCRIPTION_BRIEF.md` tells every transcription agent to "DERIVE the MCQ answer by solving".** So on any chapter whose config carries an MCQ key, an agent that reads the config entry (which is the normal instruction — the entry holds the block inventory and the canonical subtopic list) is contaminated before it derives anything. Blast radius for (a) and (b) is comment text only; (c) touches a shipped pipeline script and deserves its own 360. | **`contentHash` SORTS the option texts but keys on the answer LETTER, and the sort destroys the pairing the letter refers to.** So two questions that print the SAME four options in a DIFFERENT order and both key (A) hash identically — even though (A) denotes a different thing in each, i.e. they have different correct answers. Surfaced 2026-08-21 with a live instance in the CBSE Class-12 board-PYQ ingest: `2025 65/7/1 Q1` and `65/7/3 Q1` both read "The given graph illustrates :" over the same four inverse-trig options and both key (A) — but 65/7/1's printed (A) is `tan⁻¹ x` above an arctan graph, and 65/7/3's printed (A) is `sec⁻¹ x` above an arcsec graph. `contentHash(stem, sorted(optionTexts), "A")` cannot see the difference. This is narrow — it needs an option SHUFFLE plus a coinciding key letter, which is exactly what a multi-series board paper does — but where it fires it is silent: the upsert drops one question and the survivor keeps a key that may be wrong for the stem a reader sees. | Fixing it means hashing the correct option's TEXT rather than its letter, in `src/lib/upload/hash.ts`. It is emphatically NOT a change to make for one question. The cheap, local containment already exists and is working: `scripts/cbse-12-pyq/commit.ts` refuses to commit a figure-dependent row whose hash collides with another paper's until the pair is adjudicated in `data/hash-collisions.json` — which is how this one was caught rather than shipped. | **Identified 2026-08-21.** Logged, not acted on. The one known instance is contained (65/7/3 Q1 held out of the commit, recorded with its evidence) and the surviving row is correct and now carries its own graph. Worth a decision only if a second pipeline hits it — at which point the honest options are a per-exam hash variant or accepting the loss, not a bank-wide re-hash. |

So a build can fail for reasons unrelated to the changeset, which is the worst property for a gate to have: it trains people to re-run rather than read. **UPDATED 2026-08-18 with a sharper hypothesis than "contention", and it changes which fix is cheapest: standalone builds pass 3/3, builds INSIDE the full gate pass only 1/3.** A standalone build meets an idle database; a gate build starts immediately after ~6 minutes of the test suite hammering that same database. If that is the mechanism, the cheapest mitigation is not touching `generateStaticParams` at all but decoupling the two stages — a pause, or accepting that `prepush`'s build is the noisy one and `needsBuild` already skips it for data-only pushes. **Also note the honest attribution: the 2026-08-18 cbse-11 batch added 925 rows and 10 landing pages, so "pre-existing, not my regression" is too generous — this was already marginal and the batch plausibly tipped it.** | The two heaviest chapters in the bank are now **Worksheets Vectors 664** and **Derivatives 531** — CLAUDE.md's Recurring-pitfalls section still records the largest as 531 against the PostgREST 1000-row cap, so that figure is stale and the real headroom is smaller than documented. Candidate remedies, none costed yet: raise `staticPageGenerationTimeout` again; lower `experimental.cpus`; or narrow `generateStaticParams`' top-40 slice so the heaviest chapters are NOT prerendered (they are the ones that time out, and they render fine on demand). One run failed instead at `next build` with `canceling statement due to statement timeout` on 4 of the 317 `/questions` prerenders — **the exact failure the 2026-08-03 entry records as FIXED** by `experimental.cpus: 4` + `staticPageGenerationTimeout: 180`, which had held at zero across two builds. | **LARGELY RESOLVED 2026-08-05 — root-caused to ONE query, not generic contention.** `queryQuestions` selected the full row shape in the query carrying the `ORDER BY`, so Postgres sorted ~22k rows at ~922 bytes each to keep 25 — an `external merge` spilling **13.9 MB per call**, and **99.4% of the entire database's temp writes** (1,097 GB lifetime). That drained the Disk IO Budget, and a throttled disk is what turned every burst into statement timeouts and ECONNRESETs. Fixed in `fa9b627` (two-phase fetch); production-verified over 14,360 calls: **0 bytes temp, mean 173 ms → 43.9 ms**. The CI half was then traced to its own cause — **hook timeouts, NOT fixture races** (2026-08-05 logs: all 2,843 assertions passed, only two suites' `beforeAll`/`afterAll` exceeded 30 s, which stranded fixtures and correctly tripped the teardown guardrail) — fixed by `hookTimeout` 30 s → 90 s in `a9b30b8`. Egress was separately measured to **builds**, not traffic (37 kB/landing page × ~317 pages ≈ 25 MB/build × 3 builds × 145 pushes/month ≈ 10.9 GB vs 11.48 GB observed) and addressed by the conditional build skip (`918f0b5`) plus batching pushes to 1–2/day. **Staging is therefore NOT recommended** — every symptom it was proposed for has been fixed at its cause, and the one failure mode that would REQUIRE it (logical fixture races across concurrent runs) does not appear anywhere in the evidence. Triggers to revisit: CI failing on non-timeout errors, egress above 5 GB after a week, or a second contributor. Superseded plan follows. (1) **Recalibrate the `/questions` prerender headroom**: the 2026-08-03 fix was tuned against a smaller `syllabus_concepts`; the retry budget in `queryQuestions` (2 retries on SQLSTATE 57014) and `staticPageGenerationTimeout` may both need raising, OR the landing-page query needs its own index review now the table is larger. (2) **The 30s vitest hook timeout is too tight for ~40 DB-integration suites against ONE shared production Supabase**, especially with concurrent sessions — `retry: 1` self-heals a single flake but not three in one run. Cheapest real fixes, in order: point the test secrets at a staging project (CLAUDE.md already suggests this "if write traffic gets noisy" — it now is); or raise `hookTimeout` for the integration suites; or serialise them. **Interim reality: `--no-verify` was used once on 2026-08-04 for `393f921`** (data + docs, zero application code, every stage green on that tree at some point, CI as backstop) — that is a symptom to fix, not a practice to adopt. |

| **geo-2016 and geo-2018 are each MISSING their `Q5(ii)` construction sub-question** — found 2026-07-27 during the answer-key cross-check; two independent agents flagged it and the stored refs confirm it (both papers jump `Q5(i)` → `Q5(iii)`) | The source paper asks Q5(ii) (a `ΔSHR ∼ ΔSVU … construct ΔSVU`-type similar-triangle construction) and the **bundled official solution answers it in full with a labelled figure**, but it was never committed — most likely dropped at transcription because a construction has no numeric answer. geo-2017 and geo-2019 both HAVE their Q5(ii), so this is **2 specific questions, not a systematic Geometry gap** (verified by full sub-question inventory on all 4 geo papers + all 3 sci1 papers, which are otherwise complete). | **Identified — awaiting permission.** 360: *scope* = 2 new questions in 2 shipped papers; *blast radius* = **purely ADDITIVE** — new rows, no existing row touched, no hash recompute, nothing downstream affected (a new `content_hash` can only collide with an identical existing question, which the commit guard would catch); *does-it-apply* = yes, and cheaply — the transcription source AND the official solution are both already in hand, so this is transcribe-two-questions, not a re-ingest; *cost* = trivial (one vision/text pass over 2 pages + a scoped `commit.ts` run); *risk/reversibility* = low, reversible by `delete … where source_file=… and question_number='Q5(ii)'`; *recommendation* = **DO** — it closes the only known coverage gap in the Class-10 Maths corpus and the answers would be source-verified from the start, unlike the rest of these papers. | **`scripts/foundation/snap-crop.ts` leaks whenever a question's STEM RESUMES BELOW ITS FIGURE — measured 2026-08-27.** Its leak ceiling is `answerY`, the first option line, which is right when the figure sits directly above the options. When text of the stem continues under the figure, that text is above the ceiling and lands inside the crop. Reproduced on two of the five NDA GAT Mock 3 figures: `reproduce-1 Q4` captured "Which of the following statements is correct regarding the labelled structures?" and `metals-2 Q23` captured the question itself, "The gas ___ than air and it is ___ in water." A fix exists and is proven on all five: `scripts/foundation/figure-rect/derive.py` takes the bbox from the PDF's OWN embedded image rect instead of inferring it from text geometry, then clamps the pad to the nearest in-column text line on each side and to `answerY` as a hard floor. It needs no whitespace gap, so the collapse case disappears. It is currently a one-off for these five and would need generalising (a manifest reader, and a vector-art path — chem Q23's figure is drawn, not embedded, though its neighbouring image happened to bound it). | **Identified 2026-08-27 while fixing the Mock 3 figures; the five ARE fixed, the TOOL is not.** Left alone deliberately: `snap-crop.ts` is shipped tooling behind a 283-crop corpus, so changing its geometry needs its own 360 and a re-verify of what it already produced, not a ride-along in a five-figure repair. |
| **A literal `$` is read as a MATH DELIMITER, so two currency signs in one field swallow the prose between them — on the website AND in the Word export. Found 2026-08-29 while fixing a UPSC row.** `MATH_PATTERN` in [parseLatex.ts](src/components/math/parseLatex.ts) is a four-way alternation whose LAST arm is `\$[^$\\n]+?\$` — i.e. a single `$` opens an inline-math zone, exactly like `\(` does. It is shared by `parseLatex` + `parseRichText` and feeds `ommlBuilder`, so one regex governs both render paths. **The arm cannot simply be deleted:** `$...$` is a LEGITIMATE, in-use delimiter — `NDA_Maths_Apr2024_QuestionBank.xlsx` Q1 uses it correctly with intact LaTeX (verified: 8 backslashes, 0 control chars). So the fix is a discrimination problem (escape handling, or a currency-shaped negative lookahead), not a removal. **Measured against the real parser: backslash-escaping does NOT work** (there is no escape handling — `\$` still pairs); `\(\$\)` works and renders a normal-width `$` via KaTeX; U+FF04 fullwidth `＄` works but changes the glyph. | **5 rows, 4 of them PUBLIC**, from a scan of all 3 long-form fields across the whole bank (only the single-`$` arm counted, after masking the legitimate `$$..$$` and `\(..\)` zones). **`NDA_GAT_Weekly_Mock_2026_T4.docx` Q18/Q19/Q20 `context`** — one shared passage, so 3 rows: `A$2 billion ( $1.4 billion; ...)` renders as `A[math: 2 billion (]1.4 billion`. **`NDA_GAT_MOCK_W09.docx` Q22 `text`** — `US$1.5 trillion ... US$3 trillion`; this is a sentence-REARRANGEMENT item, so the corruption lands inside part Q, which the student must order. Plus `csp-p2.pdf` Q74 (UPSC 2019-p2, PRIVATE, fixed in its own pass). **Two distinct fixes, and they have different blast radii:** repairing the 4 rows' text changes `content_hash`, forcing delete-and-re-commit (fresh ids, orphaning any paper ref); fixing the PARSER touches every exam's web and Word render at once and needs its own regression pass. A one-line probe is committed as the detector. | **Identified 2026-08-29; NOT fixed.** Shipped work + a shared renderer, so it needs a 360 + explicit permission. Note a false lead already burned once: `NDA_Maths_Apr2024_QuestionBank.xlsx` Q1 LOOKS corrupt in a console dump (backslashes vanish in terminal output) and is intact in the DB — verify from stored bytes, not from a printed scan. |

| **The MH State Board Class-10 textbook layer was ingested by 30 parallel agents on 2026-08-28, and four inconsistencies survive that are cheap to fix but touch SHIPPED rows.** (a) **Ref style is split six-and-six across the twelve Mathematics chapters** - `AGENT_BRIEF.md` said `Ex 1.1 Q3` where the older `MATHS_TRANSCRIPTION_BRIEF.md` and the shipped `pythagoras-10` say `Ex 1.1 Q.3`, and both documents were in play at once. (b) **`config.ts` carries near-duplicate subtopics** the ingest deliberately reused rather than renamed - Geometry has both `Property of an Angle Bisector of a Triangle` and `Property of Angle Bisector of a Triangle` (1 PYQ row each), and `Construction of an Angle Bisector` beside `Construction of Angle Bisector`; Mensuration has `Area of Combined Figures` beside `Areas of Combined Figures`; Heat has `Humidity` beside `Humidity and Relative Humidity` and `Anomalous Behaviour of Water` beside `Anomalous Expansion of Water`; Refraction has `Refractive Index` beside `Absolute Refractive Index`. (c) **Several configured subtopics receive ZERO textbook rows because the chapter no longer teaches them** - Financial Planning's `Income Tax - Assessment Year` and `Income, Expenditure and Savings` (this edition is GST + shares), Life Processes Part 1's seven osmosis/transpiration/transport/excretion/glands names (the chapter covers three topics, not ten), Co-ordinate Geometry's `Equation of a Line`, Mensuration's `Euler's Formula`, Trigonometry's `Angle in Standard Position`, Carbon Compounds' `Rancidity`, Environmental Management's `Control of Noise Pollution` and `Blue Revolution`, Heredity's `Mendel's Laws of Inheritance`. All are PYQ-era names from an older syllabus. (d) **Two questions have no good subtopic home at all** - Lenses `Ex Q5` (astronomical telescope, filed under `Simple Microscope`) and Mensuration `PS7 Q12` (a chord-property question filed under `Circumference of a Circle`). | **(a) is measured and mechanical**: 383 rows across 41 files, `normalise-maths-refs.ts` is written, dry-run and self-tested, and `question_number` sits outside `content_hash` while sections routing keys on the block prefix - so the rename moves nothing and `backfill-sections.ts` re-proves every ref still routes. It waits only so it cannot race an in-flight agent. **(b) and (c) are a RENAME/RE-FILE pass and are the expensive half**: every one of those names carries live board-PYQ rows, so a merge or rename re-files SHIPPED PYQ content, which is exactly what the ingest policy refused to do unilaterally. Note the asymmetry recorded on the CDS GK run - an ADDITION is cheaper the earlier it lands, but a RENAME costs the same whenever it is paid, so waiting for a settled corpus is correct rather than merely convenient. **(d) needs a taxonomy decision, not a fix**: a `Telescopes and Optical Instruments` subtopic under Lenses would be a genuine addition; `PS7 Q12`'s real home is a chord property that Mensuration has no slot for. | **Identified 2026-08-28 during the ingest; (a) READY and pending only the last agents, (b)(c)(d) NOT done.** Each was reported by the agent that hit it rather than acted on, because `config.ts` was off-limits to them by design. (b)-(d) need a 360 + permission: they touch shipped PYQ rows and a subtopic rename is visible on `/browse` filters and every notes/guide cross-reference that names one. |

## 2026-08-28

### Measure NDA's identical awards catch-all — 13 rows, shipped and PUBLIC

The CDS GK awards subtopic was split on 2026-08-28: `Civilian Awards, Honours and Educational
Institutions` held 18 rows doing two jobs — 14 awards, 3 pure institution-identification rows
that never mention an award, 1 event — so it was renamed `Civilian Awards and Honours` and the
three institutions re-filed onto `National Institutions, Milestones and History`, which already
existed. **NDA carries a subtopic of the identical name, under an identically-named chapter,
holding 13 rows of its own**, because the CDS catalog was generated from NDA's GAT-GK taxonomy.
It was verified as a separate row (different subtopic under a different chapter under a
different subject under a different exam) and deliberately left untouched.

**Why:** if 3 of CDS's 18 were institutions filed under an awards subtopic, NDA's 13 very
likely contain some too — and NDA is live, published content students are already filtering.
The two exams now also diverge on this subtopic name, which is harmless but is a real
difference rather than an oversight, and someone should know it is deliberate. Nobody has read
NDA's 13.

**How to apply:** read all 13 and report the awards/institutions split — measurement only, no
writes. If the split mirrors CDS's, the fix is the same shape (`rename-awards.ts` is
parameterisable: it resolves its target through exam → subject → chapter and asserts exactly
one match, so pointing it at NDA is a config change, not a rewrite). Per the learning-
propagation protocol this is shipped work, so it needs a 360 + explicit permission before any
write.

### `npm run audit:text` cannot run on a large corpus — it dies on the anon statement timeout

Running `audit:text -- "CDS GK"` against the 2,280-row corpus on 2026-08-28 failed with
`canceling statement due to statement timeout`. The four text-defect classes had to be checked
by hand-written SQL instead. This is the same defect `audit:underlines` had and had fixed on
2026-08-25 — it connects as `anon` (3,000 ms cap) rather than `service_role` (8 s), and its
scan is unindexed.

**Why:** the CLAUDE.md commands table says to run this after every ingest, and it is the only
gate covering LITERAL_NEWLINE, TABLE_NO_SEPARATOR, DROPPED_SYMBOL and OPTION_LEAK. A gate that
times out on anything large is a gate nobody runs — and the corpora keep growing, so the
failure will spread rather than stay confined to CDS GK. Worse, the failure is loud enough to
be dismissed as environmental rather than read as "this corpus was never audited".

**How to apply:** port the `audit:underlines` fix — connect service-role, keep the existing
source-substring filter, and consider paging. Then re-run it against `CDS GK` to confirm the
corpus is genuinely clean rather than clean-by-hand-SQL. Note the hand SQL used on the day had
its own bug worth avoiding: in Postgres **backslash is LIKE's default ESCAPE character**, so a
literal-newline probe written `text LIKE '%\n%'` matches every row containing the letter *n*
(it reported 2,253 of 2,280 as corrupt); use `position(chr(92)||'n' in text) > 0`.

### Browser-verify the 2,280 published CDS General Knowledge rows

The corpus went PUBLIC on 2026-08-28 with no render verification of any kind. Every check was
data-level: `verify.ts` on all 19 papers, catalog conformance, control characters, math-delimiter
balance, table separators. Nothing has been looked at in a browser.

**Why:** this corpus carries shapes the data checks cannot judge — GFM pipe tables for every
Match List (dozens of them), `[Diagram: …]` bracketed descriptions standing in for two printed
maps, LaTeX in physics and chemistry stems, and a `pyq_note` provenance clause that is now
student-visible on all 2,280 rows and has never been seen rendered. The provenance clause
matters most: it is the thing telling a reader the answers are derived rather than official, and
if it renders badly or is truncated, the disclosure silently fails. Related to the existing open
item for the Batch J worksheet rows (2026-08-14) — same class, different corpus.

**How to apply:** open `/browse` filtered to CDS → General Knowledge and spot-check ~15 rows
spanning the range: a Match List (2016-2 Q34), a map question (2017-2 Q82 or Q101), a
statement-code question, a LaTeX-bearing physics row (2019-1 Q62 carries `\neq`), and any row's
answer reveal to confirm the provenance clause reads correctly. Also confirm the derived-answer
note appears on the Word answer-key export, which is a different render path.

## 2026-08-22

### `/browse` is hitting the same 8s statement-timeout wall — and unlike the dashboard, this one CAN reach students

**MEASURED 2026-08-22 — the investigation is DONE, do not repeat it.** Deferred on the user's call ("leave it"): nobody has complained, and unlike the dashboard bug it is intermittent rather than a coin flip. What it currently costs is CI-gate flakes.

Production, `pg_stat_statements` over the 3 days to 2026-08-22 — the `/browse` phase-A id query (`exam_id` + `question_kind`, `ORDER BY created_at DESC`):

| Caller | Calls | Mean | Max | Cap |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| authenticated | 402 | **1,894 ms** | **7,460 ms** | 8,000 ms |
| anon | 758 | 531 ms | 2,439 ms | 3,000 ms |
| anon, no exam filter | — | — | **2,229 ms** (53,488 index entries read for 25 rows) | 3,000 ms |

**THE CAUSE IS THE SORT, and that is isolated, not inferred.** Same query, same filters, only the ORDER BY varying (anon, JEE, `question_kind='pyq'`):

| Sort | Plan | Buffers | Time |
|---|---|---:|---:|
| *(none)* | Index Only Scan, 25 rows read | 30 | **0.2 ms** |
| `id ASC` | PK scan, no sort | 136 | 88.7 ms |
| `created_at DESC` (shipped) | scan **10,493** rows + top-N sort | 2,242 | 7.6 ms - 2,229 ms |
| `pyq_year DESC` | scan 10,493 rows + top-N sort | 8,118 | **3,116 ms** |
| **The LWS mock `.docx` ingest turned en-dashes into `--` in text and left U+2013 inside math zones — measured 2026-08-29 while building Blueprint Mock 3.** A student sees an option printed as `-- A`; inside a math zone KaTeX renders U+2013 in warn mode, so it LOOKS like a minus on screen and nothing downstream complains. `render-check-paper` reports PASS and only emits a `strict:'warn'` line, which is why this survived every gate: it is a warning, not a failure. It is a pandoc conversion artifact, not a transcription error, so it is uniform and mechanically detectable. | **83 of the 1,694 rows ingested from `NDA_Maths_%Mock%` files (4.9%)** — 40 stems, 33 options, 45 solutions. Cheap and low-risk in principle: no answer moves, and every instance seen so far is unambiguously a minus (or, once, a hyphen in `non -- empty`). But `content_hash` covers stem AND option text, so a repair changes a row's identity — it needs the expect-then-set primitive plus a recomputed hash, and any row already placed in a paper must keep its id, so this is an UPDATE with a fresh hash, never delete-and-re-commit. Three of the 83 were repaired in-scope for Mock 3 (`scripts/reviews/apply-mock3-glyph-fixes.ts` is the working pattern); the other 80 are untouched. **A blanket `--` -> `-` sweep would be wrong** — `non -- empty` needs the spaces removed too, so the replacement is context-dependent and each row needs a look. | **Identified 2026-08-29; NOT swept.** Shipped content, so it needs a 360 + explicit permission. Worth pairing with a check of whether the same artifact reached the other `.docx`-sourced corpora, which was not measured. |
| **A structural option check compares option TEXT and cannot see that two differently-written options denote the SAME thing — bit on 2026-08-29.** An NDA 2018 PYQ (`NDA1_2018_Maths_PYQ.xlsx` Q6, Sets & Relations) offers `(A u B) - (A n B)` at C and `(A' u B') - (A' n B')` at D; De Morgan makes the second `(A n B)' - (A u B)'`, which IS the first. Options A and B are likewise the same set as each other. Four distinct strings, four options, TWO correct answers — and `nopt=4`, `ncorr=1`, `ndist=4` and `audit:keys` are all green on it, because none of them evaluates the mathematics. It was caught only because a blind solver was asked to name the option matching its derived value and found two. | **One confirmed row, and the real count is unknown.** The question is live and PUBLIC in the bank; it was swapped OUT of Mock 3 rather than altered, since no key repair fixes a question with two right answers and the printed UPSC paper is what it is. The bank convention for a printed-paper defect is to PRESERVE the official key and say so in the solution, which would be hash-neutral (`content_hash` excludes `solution`) — but that is an edit to shipped content. **No probe exists for this class**: detecting it needs symbolic or exhaustive evaluation per question type (sets: exhaust a 4-element universe; algebra: sample at discriminating points), which is a real piece of tooling, not a SQL predicate. | **Identified 2026-08-29; NOT fixed and NOT measured bank-wide.** Two separable asks: (a) add an honest ambiguity note to this one row's solution, (b) build an equivalence probe and measure the class. Both need a 360 + permission. Until then the blind re-derivation pass is the only thing that catches it. |

**Why migration 0077's index does not help, despite being exactly the right shape** (`(question_kind, exam_id, created_at DESC, source_row, id) WHERE visibility='PUBLIC'`, confirmed `indisvalid`): **PostgreSQL never turns the `question_kind =` equality into an Index Cond — it is always a Filter.** Verified three ways, including a probe where `question_kind` was the ONLY predicate (still a Filter; 53,488 entries scanned for 25 rows). Because the index's LEADING column is never pinned to one value, index order is not `created_at` order, so the sort cannot be skipped. **Root cause of that refusal is NOT established** — it is an enum column, the index is valid, and the leading column is correct. That is the one open question.

Second finding: **authenticated is ~3.6x worse than anon** because a signed-in user may see PRIVATE rows, so no `WHERE visibility='PUBLIC'` partial index is usable at all. It falls back to `questions_filter_idx` (10,336 buffers).

**How to apply — and the sequencing matters more than the fix.** Reordering the sort is a PRODUCT change, not a performance fix: every candidate ordering needs its own index, `pyq_year DESC` (the most useful to a student) currently measures WORST, and `id ASC` only looks cheap here because this filter matches ~18% of the table — on a selective filter (a 15-question chapter) that plan walks the entire primary key and is far worse. So **decide the ordering BEFORE building the index, or you will build one for an order you are about to change.** Then either lead the index with `exam_id` (which the planner demonstrably does use as an Index Cond) or move `question_kind` into the index PREDICATE rather than a key column, sidestepping the enum problem entirely. Cost either way: another index on the most heavily-written table in the schema, paid on every ingest — this project has declined that trade before, deliberately. Before committing, get the usage shape: the filter split is known (48% no filters, 31% exam-only, 11% chapter/subtopic) but not WHO — if `/browse` is mostly staff building papers, newest-first may simply be correct and the whole question is moot.

### `get_format_mix` is being called ~17 times a day by something that no longer exists in `src/`

Migration 0079 demoted `get_format_mix()` to service-role-only. The logs show **`permission denied for function get_format_mix` (SQLSTATE 42501) firing 17 times on 2026-08-22**, spread across most hours of the day (02:00, 03:00, 04:00, 06:00, 08:00, 11:00-16:00). A grep of `src/`, `scripts/` and `tests/` finds **no caller at all** — the only hits are the two migration files.

**Why:** it is harmless in itself (the former call site caught the error and fell through to "mix unavailable", which is exactly why nobody noticed) — but *something is running code that no longer exists in the repo*. The likely explanations are a stale Vercel deployment still receiving traffic, or a preview deployment that was never torn down. Either would mean other retired behaviour is also still live, and that is the part worth knowing.

**How to apply:** check Vercel for old production/preview deployments still serving. The spread across hours argues against a cron and for either steady low traffic or a warm serverless instance holding an old bundle. If nothing turns up there, add a temporary `raise log` inside the function recording `current_setting('request.jwt.claims', true)` and `application_name` to identify the caller. Do not "fix" it by re-granting EXECUTE — 0079 revoked it deliberately, because at ~4.4s against the anon 3s timeout the function could never have succeeded from a request path anyway.

### Build the MHT-CET Physics and Chemistry guides — the template call is already measured for both

MHT-CET Maths shipped as Template C (tier-style strands) on 2026-08-22. Its two sibling subjects have comparable banks — Physics 2,221 PYQ / 24 chapters, Chemistry 2,165 / 30 — and the analysis that chose Maths's template already measured both, so neither needs a fresh survey to START. They must NOT be copies of the Maths guide: the three subjects have genuinely different shapes.

**Why:** Physics and Chemistry are Paper II (1 mark each) against Maths's Paper I (2 marks), so together they are half the exam and currently have no guide at all. The measurements are perishable — they were taken 2026-08-22 and the bank grows.

**How to apply:** Chemistry is the cleaner call and the better pilot — %HARD is flat AND near-zero (3.3% overall; of 30 chapters only two exceed 15% and both are tiny), which is the textbook **pure Template B** entry condition, and 5 chapters already have shipped `/notes` to cross-link. Physics is **Template C** like Maths: 17 of 24 chapters exceed 15% HARD and the HARD is concentrated (top-2 subtopics carry 63-95% where a chapter has >=4 subtopics — Wave Optics 93%, Rotational Dynamics 90%), which is a live cherry-pick pattern. Both inherit the no-negative-marking axis: at 1 mark per question and 50 questions in the shared 90-minute paper, the order-and-time framing matters even more than it does for Maths. Reuse the two agent contracts from the Maths build (a data brief carrying every verified number, and a routes brief) — they are the reason that build had no fabricated statistics.

### Guides have no subject-level registry, so every new exam hub is hand-written

`/notes` derives its hubs from `NOTES_CHAPTERS`, which is why a new exam's notes hub costs nothing. Guides have no equivalent: each hub hardcodes its own `GUIDES` array (NDA 10 entries, MHT-CET 1), which is exactly why shipping MHT-CET required a hand-written `/guide/mht-cet/page.tsx`. The 2026-08-22 rail helper `src/lib/guide/guidesNav.ts` models the EXAM level only and says so in its header.

**Why:** it is the difference between "a third exam's guides are a registry entry" and "a third exam's guides are another bespoke page". It also blocks two things that would otherwise be cheap: a rail that shows subjects under the active exam, and a data-driven check that every shipped guide subtree is actually linked from its hub.

**How to apply:** add a `GUIDE_SUBJECTS` registry (exam slug, subject route, display name, template kind, q-count source) mirroring `NOTES_CHAPTERS`' role, then rewrite both hubs to render from it and extend `guidesNav.ts` with a `subjectsForExam()`. Worth doing BEFORE the Physics/Chemistry guides above, since those would add two more hardcoded entries to a list that should be data. Keep the per-guide editorial copy where it is — only the enumeration needs to move.

### `tagNames.ts`'s principle map is exam-blind — latent today, live the moment a non-NDA guide adds principle tags

`PRINCIPLE_BY_SLUG` in `src/lib/links/tagNames.ts` is a flat `slug -> name` map built ONLY from `nda-maths/_data/principles.ts`. `question_principle_tags` is `(question_id, principle_slug)` with a free-text slug and no exam column, so if a second exam ever defines a principle with a slug NDA also uses, an MHT-CET question's `/browse` chip would render NDA's name and link into the NDA guide.

**Why:** it is a wrong cross-exam link, not a cosmetic miss, and it is invisible until it happens. It did NOT fire on 2026-08-22 only because MHT-CET Maths shipped as Template C, which has no principle axis — a deliberate choice, not a safeguard. The notes half of exactly this bug WAS live (a same-named subtopic across two exams produced a 404 for 9 NDA questions, fixed the same day in `a76f851`); the guide half is the same defect still armed.

**How to apply:** key the map by `(examName, subjectName, slug)` the way `notesIndex`/`subtopicSlugRegistry` were keyed on 2026-08-22, and take the exam from the `ResourceInput` already threaded into `getQuestionResources`. Cheap now (one exam contributes principles); it gets expensive once a second catalog exists. Note the MHT-CET Maths lever survey DID confirm 5 genuine cross-chapter principles (perpendicularity-condition 83 q/7 ch, angle-between 87/7, extremum 115/16, tangent-normal 54/7, parallelism-condition 43/5) — so a future Template-A-style principles axis for that guide is plausible, and would trip this.

## 2026-08-17

### ARCHITECTURE.md's `scripts/` tree is missing 15 of the pipelines it is supposed to map — **MAHARASHTRA GROUP DONE 2026-08-17, 10 remain**

> **Progress 2026-08-17:** `ncert/` plus the whole Maharashtra family are now documented — `mh-sb-9/`, `mh-sb-11/`, `mh-ssc-10/`, `mh-ssc-10-text/`, `mh-hsc-12-pyq/`. **Remaining 10:** `neet/`, `worksheets/`, `pariksha/`, `syllabus/`, `grounding/`, `mocks/`, `dbhealth/`, `backup/`, `quiz/`, `testdb/`. Suggested next grouping — **ops** (`dbhealth`, `backup`, `testdb`, `mocks`; all small, 4 of 4 have READMEs) then **ingestion+tooling** (`neet`, `worksheets`, `pariksha`, `grounding`, `quiz`, and `syllabus` last — at 40 scripts it is the one that may want sub-bullets rather than a single paragraph). The five entries just written are the template: purpose · what the pure core is and whether it is shared · the step scripts in runbook order · the one gotcha that earned a comment · the `[[memory-link]]`.

CLAUDE.md's file-layout section says outright: *"Append a new file/component/route there, not here."* So `ARCHITECTURE.md` is the authoritative map. Its `scripts/` tree documents `jee/`, `reviews/`, `relevance/`, `practice/`, `cds/`, `foundation/`, `practice-paper/`, `stateboard/` and the loose top-level scripts in careful detail — and **omits 15 directories entirely**: `ncert/` (added this run), `mh-sb-9/`, `mh-sb-11/`, `mh-ssc-10/`, `mh-ssc-10-text/`, `mh-hsc-12-pyq/`, `neet/`, `worksheets/`, `pariksha/`, `syllabus/`, `grounding/`, `mocks/`, `dbhealth/`, `backup/`, `quiz/`, `testdb/`.

Between them those cover the bank's two largest exams (Worksheets 7,376 q; JEE is documented but NEET 1,536 q is not), the entire syllabus-map feature, the DB-health tracker, the backup runbook and the Quiz Factory. Several ARE described in CLAUDE.md prose or in a memory, so the information is not lost — but the file that claims to be the map is ~60% complete on that section, which quietly undermines the "append there, not here" rule and pushes detail back into CLAUDE.md.

**Why:** a new session reading ARCHITECTURE.md to orient itself will conclude those pipelines don't exist, or will re-derive their shape from source. It also means the one habit CLAUDE.md relies on to stay lean isn't actually being followed.

**How to apply:** one dense paragraph each, in the existing style (purpose · the pure core · the step scripts in runbook order · the gotcha that earned a comment · the `[[memory-link]]`). Cheapest source for each is its own memory file plus its `README.md` where one exists. Do it in two or three sittings rather than one — and consider whether `scripts/` has outgrown a single tree and wants a short index with per-pipeline subsections.

### ~~The `/board` reader has never been click-verified for the 6 new NCERT chapters (29 diagrams, 4 figures)~~ — **DONE 2026-08-17** (user-verified in browser)

Every headless gate passes — `board:lint` green over 8,175 rows, all 537 rows carrying `section_seq`, `audit:text`/`audit:omml` clean — but `/board` reveals a model answer **on click**, so nothing yet run proves the answer body lays out. This batch is the first on this pipeline to ship `solution_image` diagrams (29) and `image_url` figures (4), both of which render only inside that reveal.

**Why:** this is the documented blind spot for click-gated UI — the build proves it compiles, never that it lays out ([[probe-must-reach-the-code]]). A malformed diagram URL, an oversized PNG or a KaTeX break inside a revealed solution would be invisible to every check that has run.

**How to apply:** open `/board/cbse-12/mathematics/<chapter>` for **Application of Integrals** and **Linear Programming** (the two diagram chapters) and **Vector Algebra** (the figure chapter), expand a section, and click through several answers — confirm the diagram renders at a sensible size, the figure appears above the right question, and the LaTeX is typeset rather than raw. Ten minutes; the three chapters cover all four new render paths.

### Measure whether any SHIPPED chapter has already lost errata brackets — the 5a defect is not new, only newly understood

2026-08-16 found that `apply-errata.ts` mirrors an EXERCISE row's `[Textbook…]` bracket into the transcription *band* fragment, which `apply-solutions.ts` does not read — so any `apply-solutions` run **after** an errata pass silently rewrites those rows without the bracket. It destroyed 9 of 17 on `probability-11` and was repaired the same session. Nothing establishes that earlier runs were immune: the same script shape exists in `scripts/stateboard` (Class 12, ~76 brackets), `scripts/mh-sb-9`, `scripts/mh-ssc-10` and `scripts/ncert`, and any chapter whose author looped back to fix a solution after writing errata would have hit it.

**Why:** the loss is invisible to every gate — `board:lint`, `audit:text`, `audit:keys` and `audit:omml` all pass with the brackets gone, because a missing erratum is not malformed, it is absent. So a shipped chapter can be carrying a book defect with no disclosure and nothing will ever say so. This is a **measurement, not a rework** — it needs no permission and no 360; only if it finds gaps does the repair become a backfill candidate (and the repair is trivial, since `apply-errata` is idempotent).

**How to apply:** for every `<id>.errata.json` in `scripts/{stateboard,mh-sb-9,mh-sb-11,mh-ssc-10,mh-ssc-10-text,ncert,mh-hsc-12-pyq}/data/`, count its entries and compare against `select count(*) from questions where source_file = '<sourceFile>' and solution like '[Textbook%'`. Equal = clean. A shortfall names exactly which refs to re-apply. Worth wiring the same comparison into `flip-public.ts` as a pre-flip warning, so the class cannot ship again — that is a ~15-line check and the only mechanical defence that exists for it.

### ~~Port the `--answers` flag from `scripts/mh-sb-9/render.ts` to the mh-sb-11 (and stateboard) copies~~ — **DONE 2026-08-16 (mh-sb-11 only)**

Shipped in `scripts/mh-sb-11/render.ts`: `--answers` reads `answersPdf`/`answerPages` and writes to `out/_answers/<id>/`, additively (only the chapter render `rmSync`s its directory), refusing loudly when a chapter has no answers block configured. The interim warning is gone from the pipeline README, replaced by the command. **Still open for `scripts/stateboard/`** — that copy is untouched.

`scripts/mh-sb-9/render.ts` takes `--answers` and rasterises a chapter's block of the end-of-book ANSWERS section into `out/_answers/<id>/`. The `mh-sb-11` copy has no such flag, so all four chapters this session rendered their answer pages with a hand-written one-off PyMuPDF call. The README now documents the gap (with the warning that re-running plain `render.ts <id>` rmSync's the chapter PNGs that in-flight transcription agents are reading) rather than closing it, because it is a shipped script and the run was mid-flight.

**Why:** the step-6 answer-key cross-check is the mandatory gate for every chapter of these books, and its input is currently produced by retyping a command. That is a per-chapter opportunity to render the wrong page range, or to reach for `render.ts <id>` by reflex and destroy the working set. Eleven Class-11 chapters remain, plus whatever Class-12 and Class-9 work follows.

**How to apply:** copy the `--answers` branch from `scripts/mh-sb-9/render.ts`, reading `answersPdf` + `answerPages` from the chapter config and writing to `out/_answers/<id>/` (a SIBLING of `out/<id>/`, never inside it). Make the flag refuse when a chapter has no `answersPdf` — the humanities chapters legitimately have none, and a silent no-op there would read as "the answers are missing". Then delete the interim warning from `scripts/mh-sb-11/README.md`.

### Port the `apply-errata.ts` MIRROR-ORDER fix to the sibling pipelines — `mh-sb-9` and `mh-sb-11` have it, four others do not

*(Related to but DISTINCT from the bracket-loss measurement above — that one is about running `apply-solutions` **after** errata; this one is about **where** the bracket lands in the first place. Both can be true of the same chapter.)*

`apply-errata.ts` mirrors a bracket into the **first** `readdirSync` match and stops. For an exercise-subjective row two source files hold the ref: the transcription fragment, which has **no `solution` key** (exercise answers are authored later), and the authored `*.solutions.json`, which does. Alphabetically the fragment usually sorts first — so the applier **creates** a `solution` field on the fragment holding the bracket ALONE, and the real solution never records it. Found and fixed in `scripts/mh-sb-11` on 2026-08-16 *after* it had already fired on three chapters (6 rows, re-mirrored from the live DB text). `scripts/mh-sb-9` took this fix earlier; `scripts/stateboard`, `scripts/mh-ssc-10`, `scripts/mh-ssc-10-text`, `scripts/ncert` and `scripts/mh-hsc-12-pyq` have not been checked.

**Why:** two independent harms, both silent. The authored solution keeps no record of the disclosure, and a later re-commit reads the fragment's bracket-only field as that question's **entire model answer** — a row that would ship a one-sentence erratum where a worked solution belongs. No gate sees either: a bracket in the wrong file is not malformed, and a fragment carrying an unexpected `solution` key is structurally valid.

**How to apply:** in each pipeline's `apply-errata.ts`, sort the candidate file list so `*.solutions.json` ranks before transcription fragments (`const rank = (f) => f.endsWith(".solutions.json") ? 0 : 1`), keeping the glob wide so newly-named fragments are still found. Then audit for damage already done: any NON-`solved` row in a `*.band-*.json` / fragment whose `solution` starts with `[Textbook` is a hit. **Do not blanket-move them** — an MCQ row has no `*.solutions.json` entry at all, so the fragment genuinely is its only home (6 of the 12 rows found in the mh-sb-11 sweep were MCQs and correctly stayed). Classify by whether a solutions file owns the ref, and take the repair text from the LIVE DB row so the mirror is byte-exact rather than reconstructed.

### Run an INDEPENDENT step-6 cross-check on the four chapters shipped 2026-08-16 (Conics · Binomial · Differentiation · Circle)

Those four were cross-checked **by the authoring pass** — each agent derived its answers, then read the printed key and reported discrepancies — rather than by a separate `dump-review.ts` pass with fresh agents. The finding was strong (0 our-answer-wrong across every keyed row, ~40 book defects surfaced), and it is stated as such in the commit message and the Decisions log rather than dressed up as an independent gate.

**Why:** the weakness is specific and worth closing rather than living with. A *systematic* error in an agent's own derivation would survive, because the same reasoning produced both the answer and the verdict on the answer — the check shares a failure mode with the thing it checks. The compensating control that DOES exist is the genuinely blind MCQ pass (48/48 triple-confirmed, zero mismatches), but that covers 48 of 472 rows; the 342 authored subjective answers have no independent second opinion. Every other chapter of this book got a separate pass.

**How to apply:** `npx tsx scripts/mh-sb-11/dump-review.ts <chapterId>` per chapter, then dispatch cross-check agents that see the stored answer and the rendered answer-page images but NOT the authoring agent's reasoning. Two scope facts to hand them so a shortfall is visible: solved examples are outside the gate by construction (rule 6a), and Binomial `Ex 4.5` has no printed key at all (it is titled "Show That" and this key never answers a proof), so the expected covered count is well below the row count. Cheapest first pass would be the ~40 rows already carrying an errata bracket, since those are where a disagreement is most likely to be real.

### Lint for merged sub-item solved rows — the convention is uniform only because an agent happened to ask

The shipped convention across every completed chapter of these textbook pipelines is that a solved example with printed sub-items `i)/ii)/iii)` becomes ONE ROW PER SUB-ITEM sharing `context` + `setLabel`. On 2026-08-16 one transcription band instead merged all sub-items inline into a single row (three examples, eleven real questions), citing a shipped precedent that a one-line query disproved — across five completed chapters, *every* sub-item ref carries a `setLabel`. It surfaced only because a **different** band flagged the divergence and asked rather than silently conforming; nothing mechanical would have caught it.

**Why:** a merged row is a worse artifact than the split — `/board` renders three questions as one, a student cannot attempt them separately, and the row's single `difficulty` and `subtopic` describe a set rather than a question. It is also invisible downstream: the rows commit, counts reconcile, and every audit passes. Relying on one agent noticing another's inconsistency is not a control.

**How to apply:** add a check to `merge.ts` (or a standalone lint alongside `board:lint`) that flags any `bucket:"solved"` row whose `stem` contains an inline sub-item marker — `(^|\s)i\)\s`, `ii)`, `iii)` — while carrying no `setLabel`. Triage, not a gate, since a stem could legitimately quote a list; but it turns a convention into something checkable. Pin the three `trigonometry-2-11` rows that were re-split as regression fixtures.
